1. the one-step rooted marked supplier bounds are finite:
                                          Km < ∞,             0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
   uniformly under the standing convention;

   associated-graded diagonal is
                                                     L4−∆ ;

                                     Γ = log2 max{Km , 1} + ε,

                                                   Γ+3

                                                    κ
   and δD → ∞.
   Then there are finite D, finite dyadic L, and an equivalent rooted marked norm such that
                                           ∥DΦL (0)|stable ∥ < 1.
No upper bound of the form maxm Km < 4 is required.
Proof. Fix the finite number Γ. Choose D so that
                                                  δD > Γ + 2.
On the finite local quotient, the diagonal eigenvalues have modulus at most L−1 , with the unmarked
scalar sector at most L−2 . Since JL is finite dimensional and triangular, for every ϵL > 0 there is an
equivalent finite-dimensional norm with
                                      ∥JL ∥ ≤ r(JL ) + ϵL ≤ L−1 + ϵL .
Choose ϵL = L−1 . Thus
                                                 ∥JL ∥ ≤ 2L−1 .
  The norm extension to the rooted space changes the displayed nonlocal constants by finite factors
depending only on the fixed D, L, never on volume or weak depth. Since
                                                  δD − Γ > 2,
the short row tends to zero as L → ∞, despite the logarithmic factor, and the long row is O(L−3 ). Choose
one finite L such that
                             2L−1 + Clong
                                     ′

                                                    (1 + R(L))pD LΓ−δD < 1.
This gives the strict macrostep contraction.

gives the graph, and the mean-value theorem gives centered contraction.

Theorem 3.9 (Corrected support closure). Assume:

Theorem 3.9 (Corrected support closure). Assume:
1. the summed replica-BKAR theorem;

                                                     χeff (u, r, b) < 1,

Proof. The first two lemmas leave a residual connector decay µ − κ > 0 and convert every fixed geometric

in the effective rooted link mass χeff . Reblocking is paid by the engineering factor and does not weaken κ.
Choose D with δD > Γ + 3, then choose finite L so qloc lies inside the strict associated-graded jet gap.
Finally shrink u, r, b to absorb the nonlinear and irregular source terms.

Theorem 4.22 (Exact Loc/collect dichotomy). For every 0 ≤ m ≤ 3, the nonlocal block NL splits exactly
into long and short rows:
                                      NL = NLlong + NLshort .
  The long row satisfies
                                             ∥NLlong ∥[m] ≤ Cm L−3 .

                                              δD = D − 3 −→ ∞.
   The estimates are uniform in periodic volume and preceding weak depth. They remain valid with one
or two passive exterior roots and every fixed finite passive source order.

Proof. For the long sector,
                                              [m]                                               [m]

   For the short sector, first apply exact collect and then the local projector. Linearity and absolute

                                              τL (Y ) ≤ R(L),

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.


                                                              34
(E2) Coarea logarithm. Append the field-dependent logarithm

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.
(E5) Canonical hull and collect. Report covariance paths and finite collars, assign each history to
     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization
     output gradient and complex Hessian.

active/source inventory.

                                                  C(uω + r) ≤ b∗
and the derived output allocation is at most b∗ . This is an invariant ball inequality, not circular use of
the output theorem as an input.

                                          Km < ∞,             0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
   uniformly under the standing convention;

                                                     L4−∆ ;

                                     Γ = log2 max{Km , 1} + ε,

                                           ∥DΦL (0)|stable ∥ < 1.
No upper bound of the form maxm Km < 4 is required.
Proof. Fix the finite number Γ. Choose D so that
                                                  δD > Γ + 2.
On the finite local quotient, the diagonal eigenvalues have modulus at most L−1 , with the unmarked
scalar sector at most L−2 . Since JL is finite dimensional and triangular, for every ϵL > 0 there is an
equivalent finite-dimensional norm with
                                      ∥JL ∥ ≤ r(JL ) + ϵL ≤ L−1 + ϵL .
Choose ϵL = L−1 . Thus
                                                 ∥JL ∥ ≤ 2L−1 .
  The norm extension to the rooted space changes the displayed nonlocal constants by finite factors
depending only on the fixed D, L, never on volume or weak depth. Since
                                                  δD − Γ > 2,
the short row tends to zero as L → ∞, despite the logarithmic factor, and the long row is O(L−3 ). Choose
one finite L such that

                                     ′

                                                    (1 + R(L))pD LΓ−δD < 1.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

forty-nine-path symbol raises degree only by even positive amounts. Thus all off-diagonal local/remainder
blocks belong to NL , while the finite local diagonal is computed before the support-envelope estimate.

Theorem 4.22 (Exact Loc/collect dichotomy). For every 0 ≤ m ≤ 3, the nonlocal block NL splits exactly
into long and short rows:
                                      NL = NLlong + NLshort .

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.

The factor log 2 is the physical scale-two interval. The complete coordinate-intertwining theorem is
printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected

and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
     ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
     pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
     scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj ), and the
     weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
     macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
     are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
     Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
     common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
     acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
     column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-

before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.

                                                     82
Lemma 8.27 (Exact profile division). At every fixed field degree, the constant-profile vector kernel of the
complete improved local score through engineering dimension four vanishes at zero profile momentum. It
therefore admits exact finite Laurent division
                                               X
                                     Vµ (p) =   (eiapα − 1)Wµα (p).                                   (148)
                                                      α

Thus the apparent factor v/a in (124) is converted before absolute root summation into a bounded discrete
derivative of v.
Proof. A zero-profile-momentum coefficient would be a local, reflection-compatible, gauge-invariant vector
of engineering dimension at most four. No such vector exists: a connection mass term is not gauge
invariant; Tr dρ(Fµν ) = 0 because g is semisimple; traces of dρ(DF ) reduce to derivatives of the same
zero trace; and two curvature two-forms cannot be contracted with invariant metric tensors while leaving
exactly one free vector index. The epsilon tensor has the wrong reflection parity. Hence the finite Laurent
polynomial Vµ (z) vanishes at z1 = · · · = z4 = 1. Successive telescoping in the variables zα writes it in the
ideal generated by zα − 1 and preserves finite range, proving (148).

The constants are independent of cutoff, finite volume, weak depth, and fine-cell count. Moreover, on the

Proof. The source is a globally smooth fixed-range function of finitely many links. On the regular weak
chart its Taylor jets satisfy the common marked-C 3 theorem, while on the complement its fixed group
derivatives enter the already established irregular activity row. Lemma 8.18 removes the vacuum Haar
trace before any support norm, and Lemma 8.19 removes the field-linear color trace. Taylor-expand the
smooth profile on each link neighborhood. Lemma 8.27 moves the potentially dangerous constant-profile
low jet onto ∇v by a signed algebraic identity. Theorem 8.26 identifies the dimension-four quotient
with the traceless stress and scalar coupling-conjugate channels; the antisymmetric part is the already
controlled rotation/total-difference row. Every other regular term contains a second profile derivative,
two additional lattice derivatives, an equation-of-motion contact, or a nonreference root. After canonical
rescaling these give Cℓj p2 (v).
    There is no source-chart singularity on the irregular complement: Ωsin and all of its group derivatives
are bounded globally. Use the same regular/irregular partition and Wilson-deficit estimate as the all-stage
local activity theorem. The exact profile division and canonical dimension separation are performed
before absolute root summation, so the explicit lattice-scale factor produces either a discrete derivative
of the profile or a dimension-six remainder. The remaining fixed local derivative losses are absorbed by
ϵirr (uj ). Root at the first source link, preserve signs through the vacuum and Laurent identities, and
then invoke Theorem 7.3 and Corollary 7.4. A second source derivative acts on finitely many local profile
factors and is controlled by the same common marked-C 3 row. Exact conditional pushforward and the
joint matching connector preserve these bounds with a fixed factor. Finally Proposition 8.21 gives the
O(a2 ) replacement error on bounded geometric representatives.

Finite incidence identities The finite clover and incidence formulas satisfy:
1. exact cancellation of the distinguished-link derivative in the two-plaquette clover;
2. exact gauge covariance under arbitrary lattice G gauge transformations;
3. midpoint/reflection cancellation showing an O(a3 ) per-link strip defect;
4. exact signed-hypercubic covariance of the linear incidence stencil;
5. the zero trace of every first nonlinear adjoint diagonal block.
These identities, together with the all-stage marked theorem and the terminal joint Haar–optical connector
established in Part III, complete the finite-cutoff source construction.

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
    (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,

        has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
   (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
        derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
        differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
        cancellation assumption.
 (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
        in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
        lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
        row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
  (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
        cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
        its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
        zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.

root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .

Compatibility of the physical scale with the common diagonal. Let f be the profile of
Lemma 10.1, and let the constants Cf , pf be those in the uniform endpoint and source-defect rows
above. Choose the fixed countable source-scale set used in Corollary 8.12 to contain a cofinal dyadic

                                                         1

                                       
                                                          2
Before taking the common diagonal subsequence, enlarge the countable source algebra by the fixed fields
Yr (f ), ΘYr (f ), and their reflected product. The same single diagonal extraction then gives all Schwinger
limits entering their OS form and preserves every cutoff- and volume-uniform lower bound. The scale r is
not changed after extraction. The cofinal dyadic choice is countable, while u(r) → 0 and r2 L(r)pf → 0
along it. Hence one r satisfies the displayed inequality. Adding finitely many fields at that scale preserves
countability of the source algebra. The source-tree theorem and Corollary 8.12 therefore permit the
same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform
finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit.

No exact Wilson pushforward, Wilson moment identity, or reducing retained Hilbert subspace is used.

Proof. The finite spectral measure has total mass

                                    0 < µY ([m⋆ , ∞)) = ∥ψY ∥2OS < ∞.

Since [m⋆ , n] ↑ [m⋆ , ∞), continuity from below implies that some finite n has positive mass. Set
MY = n.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
                                               11N log 2
                             u(2L) = u(L) +              u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ).
                                                 24π 2
No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
not omitted conclusions.

A     Endpoint packet-shell verification

uses the standing uniformity convention of Section 1. The main text isolates the logical endpoint
theorem; the statements below retain the full finite-volume, crossing-cell, pre-owner, compact-entrance,
and terminal-shell verification needed for that theorem.

Proposition 6.13 (Finite-volume Perron input from two-scale landing). For every finite spatial volume,

The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates
                            Wγ = Pγ (U ),      Zγ,k = Uek , 2 ≤ k ≤ L,
with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.

                                L

Proof. For fixed details, Ue1 7→ Wγ is right multiplication by a fixed group element. Haar invariance
proves the identity. Edge-disjointness tensorizes it, gauge transformations telescope, and reflection reverses
and inverts the product.

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

prescribed finite source orders QA , QB , first collect every source contact subtree contained in A or B into
                                                  (Q )         (Q )
its complete normalized local coefficient. Let RA A and RB B denote the resulting bounded positive-scale

                                        (Q )   (Q )

with the same exponent mE as Theorem 6.43. The constant may depend on the two fixed local source

                                                         70
Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each
complete contact cluster before the matching collect and bound it in one finite local root norm. Insert

nonroot activity row, incidence-split coverage allocation, multi-pivot nuclear closure estimate, and strict

only CQA ,QB and not the connector exponent.

The same inequalities hold componentwise for a fixed finite family of bounded tensor sources. For
a renormalized local-field source, the geometric averaging is followed by its finite triangular source-
renormalization matrix. Those matrices are treated in the local root norm; after the declared source
normalization their products are bounded on every fixed finite jet family and do not alter the connector
exponent.
Proof. Each coarse value is a convex average of the fine values, proving the first inequality. The second
follows by the triangle inequality and the partition of the fine cells into coarse cells.

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
     ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
     pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
     scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj ), and the
     weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
     macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
     are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
     Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
     common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
     acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
     column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.

matching exponent of Theorem 6.43. Both quantities are fixed after the first-crossing section and the
finite macrobridge are fixed; neither depends on microscopic spacing or ultraviolet depth.

Proof. The first-crossing value, the weak threshold, and the finite macrobridge parameters are fixed

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.

                                                     82
Lemma 8.27 (Exact profile division). At every fixed field degree, the constant-profile vector kernel of the
complete improved local score through engineering dimension four vanishes at zero profile momentum. It
therefore admits exact finite Laurent division
                                               X
                                     Vµ (p) =   (eiapα − 1)Wµα (p).                                   (148)
                                                      α

Thus the apparent factor v/a in (124) is converted before absolute root summation into a bounded discrete
derivative of v.
Proof. A zero-profile-momentum coefficient would be a local, reflection-compatible, gauge-invariant vector
of engineering dimension at most four. No such vector exists: a connection mass term is not gauge
invariant; Tr dρ(Fµν ) = 0 because g is semisimple; traces of dρ(DF ) reduce to derivatives of the same
zero trace; and two curvature two-forms cannot be contracted with invariant metric tensors while leaving
exactly one free vector index. The epsilon tensor has the wrong reflection parity. Hence the finite Laurent
polynomial Vµ (z) vanishes at z1 = · · · = z4 = 1. Successive telescoping in the variables zα writes it in the
ideal generated by zα − 1 and preserves finite range, proving (148).

Displacement-root closure Let e0 (v), ω(v) = 14 ∂ · v, and a(v) denote the symmetric traceless, scalar,
and antisymmetric parts of ∂µ vν .
Theorem 8.28 (Explicit clover displacement-root theorem). Insert the source (124) before the complete
normalized conditional logarithm. On the common weak domain, after exact weak/irregular splitting and
transport to the joint Haar–optical matching logarithm,

                          Tj (v) = Tj0 (e0 (v)) + Θj (ω(v)) + Aj (a(v)) + Rdisp

The regular one-displacement-mark remainder obeys

                               Rdisp
                                j    (v)              ≤ Cℓj p2 (v) + Cϵirr (uj )p1 (v),                 (150)
                                           root,[2]


and the corresponding two-displacement-mark row obeys

                                   Dv2 Rdisp
                                        j    (v1 )                   ≤ Cp2 (v1 )p2 (v2 ).               (151)
                                                          root,[2]

The constants are independent of cutoff, finite volume, weak depth, and fine-cell count. Moreover, on the
bounded geometric source core, replacing the exact anchored displacement by X cl changes every matrix
element by O(a2 ) before the continuum limit.
Proof. The source is a globally smooth fixed-range function of finitely many links. On the regular weak
chart its Taylor jets satisfy the common marked-C 3 theorem, while on the complement its fixed group
derivatives enter the already established irregular activity row. Lemma 8.18 removes the vacuum Haar
trace before any support norm, and Lemma 8.19 removes the field-linear color trace. Taylor-expand the
smooth profile on each link neighborhood. Lemma 8.27 moves the potentially dangerous constant-profile
low jet onto ∇v by a signed algebraic identity. Theorem 8.26 identifies the dimension-four quotient
with the traceless stress and scalar coupling-conjugate channels; the antisymmetric part is the already
controlled rotation/total-difference row. Every other regular term contains a second profile derivative,
two additional lattice derivatives, an equation-of-motion contact, or a nonreference root. After canonical
rescaling these give Cℓj p2 (v).
    There is no source-chart singularity on the irregular complement: Ωsin and all of its group derivatives
are bounded globally. Use the same regular/irregular partition and Wilson-deficit estimate as the all-stage
local activity theorem. The exact profile division and canonical dimension separation are performed
before absolute root summation, so the explicit lattice-scale factor produces either a discrete derivative
of the profile or a dimension-six remainder. The remaining fixed local derivative losses are absorbed by
ϵirr (uj ). Root at the first source link, preserve signs through the vacuum and Laurent identities, and
then invoke Theorem 7.3 and Corollary 7.4. A second source derivative acts on finitely many local profile
factors and is controlled by the same common marked-C 3 row. Exact conditional pushforward and the
joint matching connector preserve these bounds with a fixed factor. Finally Proposition 8.21 gives the
O(a2 ) replacement error on bounded geometric representatives.

                                        Ea [δvgeo Fa ] = Ea [Fa Ta (v)] + O(a2 ),                      (152)

with the same statement after passage through the exact projective conditional system. Along the common

projective limits of Tj0 and Θj .

Proof. Apply finite-dimensional Haar integration by parts to X cl and use Proposition 8.21 on the left-hand
side. Theorem 8.28 supplies uniform integrability and projective transport on the right.

Finite incidence identities The finite clover and incidence formulas satisfy:
1. exact cancellation of the distinguished-link derivative in the two-plaquette clover;
2. exact gauge covariance under arbitrary lattice G gauge transformations;
3. midpoint/reflection cancellation showing an O(a3 ) per-link strip defect;
4. exact signed-hypercubic covariance of the linear incidence stencil;
5. the zero trace of every first nonlinear adjoint diagonal block.
These identities, together with the all-stage marked theorem and the terminal joint Haar–optical connector
established in Part III, complete the finite-cutoff source construction.

Theorem 8.33 (Projective stress Schwinger distribution). Along the common directed subsequence, the
limit
                           W(f ; A1 , . . . , An ) = lim Wj (f ; A1 , . . . , An )            (159)
                                                         j→∞

exists simultaneously for the countable rational Schwartz/source core and extends uniquely by continuity.
It is:
(S1) linear and Schwartz tempered in f ;
(S2) multilinear and continuous in bounded exterior cylinders;
(S3) local relative to the common gauge-invariant source system;
(S4) continuously translation covariant and signed-hypercubic covariant;
(S5) independent of the compatible microscopic representative.
Proof. Apply (154) between consecutive resolutions. The exact finite cocycle moves the retained leading

difference by the summable sequence (156). Thus every matrix element is Cauchy. The uniform tree bound
gives a finite Schwartz seminorm and permits diagonal extraction on the countable core. Translation
continuity, locality, clustering, and representative independence follow from the uniform rooted source-tree
bound and the summable recurrence above. The tensor source is signed-hypercubic at finite cutoff. Its
complete filtered low-jet block is normalized by Theorem 8.24; the remaining antisymmetric regulator
response is two engineering dimensions higher after one or two geometric marks. The angular Ward
theorem below then integrates the resulting infinitesimal action to O(4) covariance.

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
    (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,
        three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
        has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
   (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
        derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
        differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
        cancellation assumption.
 (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
        in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
        lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
        row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
  (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
        cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
        its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
        zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .
Proof. Appendix A gives the full proof inside this manuscript. Its five subsections establish the grounded-
torus covariance and globally subtracted packet division; the weak crossing recurrence and wrapping rows;
the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact-
entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
                                               11N log 2
                             u(2L) = u(L) +              u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ).
                                                 24π 2
No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
not omitted conclusions.

localized-wrapping and massless acoustic finite-size term.
   The Wick associated-graded theorem, permanent envelope, localization/collect dichotomy, macrostep
contraction, and graph transform give U3. The bad-seed and rooted irregular-transfer theorems give U4.
The bi-graded marked theorem gives U5. Exact algebra, reflected section, ownership, and recombination
give U6. Finally, U2, U3, domain compactness, and the directed schedule give U7.

6     Directed handoff and higher-order Peter–Weyl landing
The purpose of this section is to replace the phrase “fixed compact first-crossing section” by a quantitative
object and to print the exact norm bound consumed by the compact-group macrobridge. Fix once and
for all

Theorem 6.6 (Compact weak first-crossing class). The class C⋆ is compact in Ba0 ,2,N and every member
satisfies the weak-reference row bounded by Mwk , uniformly in finite volume, cutoff, exterior configuration,
and preceding weak depth. No Haar-relative smallness is asserted by this theorem.
Proof. The family is bounded in the stronger Ba,3,N topology. The support reserve e−(a−a0 )|X| and
Arzelà–Ascoli on the finitely many bounded rooted shapes give compactness from C 3 to C 2 .
Lemma 6.7 (Reporting completeness and depth telescoping). Let µj be the exact retained measure after
j weak steps, before the next logarithm is taken. For every finite depth n there is an exact representation
                                                   reg
                             dµj = Zj−1 exp{−Qref
                                              j − Rj } dHj + Ij ,                                  0 ≤ j ≤ n,   (HT1)

where dHj is product Haar on the retained links, Qref  j is the complete allocated scalar/two-jet reference,
Rjreg is the collected regular residual, and Ij is the recombined irregular operator. Every pre-logarithmic
factor of the exact step occurs in exactly one of these three rows, and the scalar normalizations satisfy
                                                                                    n−1
                                                                                    Y
                                        Zj+1 = Zj zj ,                Zn = Z0              zj ,                 (HT2)
                                                                                    j=0

with no zj reappearing as a nonempty-support activity. The representation and its zero/one/two marked
versions are uniform in depth.
Proof. The assertion is proved by induction on j. At j = 0, the Wilson–Haar measure and the exact
inner/collar/outer partition give (HT1): the complete quadratic reference is Qref
                                                                              0 , the post-two-jet inner
density is the regular row, and the recombined collar/outer/curvature labels are I0 .
   Assume (HT1) at step j. Apply the exact joint Gaussian/Karcher disintegration before taking a
connected logarithm. DAS3 retains the scalar normalization of that one joint law once at the exterior
endpoint; call it zj . The empty-support part therefore multiplies Zj and gives (HT2). All nonempty
factors are first reported on their actual local support or on the canonical hull of their exponential


                                                                54
connector. The factor inventory is exhaustive: Wilson words, product-Haar coordinate factors, coarea
and normal returns, gauge-coordinate endpoints, Gaussian/Schur endpoints, inherited activities, cutoffs,
and source leaves are exactly the ownership inventory in row (U1) of Theorem 5.27, implemented by
the one-step maps (E1)–(E6), together with the labelled inner/collar/outer partition. The labels sum
pointwise to one before logarithms, so an atlas-boundary factor cannot be lost between branches.
   Definition 3.22 assigns every reported nonempty factor to precisely one of the regular, one-core, or
cross rows; Lemma 3.25 does the same for every connected determinant word. Exact collect then replaces
each history by one canonical hull. The history-compatibility theorem computes the next allocation only
from this collected output, so neither a factor nor its scalar normalization is copied at a later depth. This
gives (HT1) at j + 1. The normalized structural/source marks commute with the finite induction and are
bounded by the same permanent rows, proving depth uniformity.

                                               X                X

The same inequalities hold componentwise for a fixed finite family of bounded tensor sources. For

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
     ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
     pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
     scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj ), and the
     weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
     macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
     are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
     Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
     common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
     acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
     column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.
   Define the quantitative rates

norm because of Lemma 8.1.

Lemma 8.5 (Matching-scale spacetime connector). For bounded matching-scale cylinders F, G supported
in physical sets A, B,
                           |Cov(F, G)| ≤ CF,G exp{−m0 dist1 (A, B)}                           (114)
with m0 > 0 independent of spatial volume and microscopic cutoff.
Proof. Use F and G as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The matching forest is four-dimensional:
its distinguished root path pays the same optical, Haar-carrier, and reported-support exponents in

                                                     74
Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write

                                     Wm (J) = log Zm (J) − log Zm (0)

for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm , . . . , Rjw and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
   A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,                                        X
                             Dn Wm (0)[J1 , . . . , Jn ] =   Am (τ ; J1 , . . . , Jn ),                 (115)
                                                      τ ∈Tm,n

where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.

    For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm (0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw .
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.

                           W(f ; A1 , . . . , An ) = lim Wj (f ; A1 , . . . , An )            (159)

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .
Theorem 6.44 (Two composite-root extension). Fix two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B. For any
prescribed finite source orders QA , QB , first collect every source contact subtree contained in A or B into
                                                  (Q )         (Q )
its complete normalized local coefficient. Let RA A and RB B denote the resulting bounded positive-scale
roots, or normalized local roots measured in the permanent finite source-renormalization norm. Then
                                        (Q )   (Q )
                                  K(RA A , RB B ) ≤ CQA ,QB e−mE dist(A,B) ,                                (105)

with the same exponent mE as Theorem 6.43. The constant may depend on the two fixed local source
orders and root types, but not on volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, or preceding weak depth.


                                                         70
Proof. The arbitrary fixed passive-source theorem and the common analytic species tube form each
complete contact cluster before the matching collect and bound it in one finite local root norm. Insert
those two coefficients as the two distinguished roots of the exact joint compact–optical logarithm; do
not represent their internal source leaves as additional bulk source marks. Every pivot and connector
lying wholly inside either fixed root buffer is absorbed into its root constant. Outside the two buffers the
nonroot activity row, incidence-split coverage allocation, multi-pivot nuclear closure estimate, and strict
branch coefficient are exactly those of Theorem 6.43. The unique connecting path therefore pays the
same spatial exponent mE . For normalized local fields the weak triangular source theorem supplies the
finite root seminorm before this matching-scale insertion. Thus arbitrary finite local source order changes
only CQA ,QB and not the connector exponent.

Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique
lowest common ancestor v⊤ ; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor.
    All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every
contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤ .
   The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.
When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector
theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity
of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared.

Lemma A.15 (Complete root-separating bridge cancellation). At any finite weak terminal logarithm,
after complete BRS/reflection, regular/irregular and normalization recombination, the complete sum of
a covariance class possessing a canonically selected root-separating bridge is zero before absolute values.
The identity remains true with zero, one or two already assigned g marks.
Proof. Contract the maximal two-edge-connected blocks and select the first bridge on the bridge-tree
path leaving the negative-time root. Deleting that bridge leaves two complete connected sides and gives
the exact coefficientwise factorization

Packet completion precedes the bridge selection, so denominator derivatives, localization contacts, source-
motion contacts and off-path branches remain inside L and R. Each side contains one scalar curvature
root and one open adjoint leg. Global color invariance makes both one-leg packets vanish. The identity
holds for the whole C 2 family in g, hence also after any distribution of at most two g marks.

entrance density before the large-field labels, bad components, canonical owners, history certificates, Fourier
